ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e3648	Deputies! In response to the report of the CPT Committee, I am not concerned solely with measures for the Federal Police Directorate Vienna, but with a bundle of measures aimed at all the security authorities and their employees. For example, I can list some of the measures we are considering: It is planned, on the one hand, to conduct a week of human rights in the first half of 1997. It will take place throughout the resort. The aim of the event is to enable public security services institutions to engage in practical discussions on fundamental rights and their importance for everyday work. Secondly, We will, in view of the high proportion of cases of abuse involving foreigners, carry out an intensive training in matters of foreign law, which will also be offered and carried out throughout the country in 1997. Thirdly, A model project will shortly be carried out in the area of the Federal Police Directorate Linz and at least one additional security department, and the documentation of the hearing of people who are being held for a serious crime will be tested by means of video recording. This is to prove that overall control of the hearing does not lead to a reduction in the meaning of the identity card. We also follow a suggestion from the CPT Committee. Fourth: In its report, the CPT assumes that, in principle, a stopper should have a right to the presence of his defender in his consent. This is also to be the subject of the model project. Fifth, you open a bottle Medical checks at the end of the safety records shall be extended to all cases where the person concerned claims a physical impairment or leads to an organ giving evidence. There is therefore a doctor’s starting or leaving examination. We also plan to set up a detention counsellor and also to meet a strong demand from the CPT. Finally, it is also a question of reducing the loads. The high number of temporary workers presents great difficulties in the area of police prisoners' homes. As part of the proposed Foreign Law Amendment Act, so-called "relieved funds" are therefore being put in place to boost. In addition, opportunities are being sought to create more prison rooms in the short term in cooperation with private builders. Finally, it is also intended to deepen cooperation between the Security Authorities and the Justice Department, since the training of police officers and gendarmen is not primarily geared to the service in police prisons and we are therefore seeking an intensified cooperation with the guard who is specialised in this task.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e4326	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00083/index.shtml -- 83/M]] As part of the so-called Integration Package (Novelisation of the Xenophobia, Residence and Asylum Act), will you also submit a draft for the revision of the Citizenship Act in order to facilitate access to citizenship in Austria as well as in France?
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e4353	Please have a reply, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e4371	Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I do not intend to amend the citizenship law in the context of the integration package. This is mainly because it is in line with the usual practice of consulting citizenship issues with the countries responsible for the enforcement of citizenship matters and, subsequently, acting in agreement with the countries. However, my intention is to discuss proposals to amend citizenship law with the countries at the next meeting of the country's citizenship representatives at official level, and I assume that an amendment can then be drawn up on the basis of this discussion. Consideration from my point of view goes in the direction of adapting the current very different practice at national level to the waiting period for citizenship throughout the country and also of allowing time-limits to be offered, for example, in the event of sufficient language control. The question of dual citizenship and the question of the easier acquisition of nationality for so-called host children or children of foreign parents who already live here in the second generation should also be considered together with the countries, and I do not intend to present a concrete proposal to the countries at first.

Those States, in particular Germany, who have been talking about forced return or repatriation from the outset, have not been able to achieve comparable successes so far. ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e5576	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e5592	Deputies! I express my thanks for this question because I feel that the use of the Austrian executive forces in this area is indeed far too little attention. Austria is currently participating in three UN missions by sending a total of 24 army or police officers. In Bosnia-Herzegovina, 18 executive officials are currently employed at UNIPTF's UN mission. Its task is to monitor local police forces and support the reconstruction of orderly security in Bosnia-Herzegovina. Particular attention will be paid to the monitoring of respect for human rights after this murderous war. A quota of four officials is used in East Slavonia. Its task is to build a police force consisting of both groups. In particular, cooperation between the Croats and Serbs will be promoted. Of course, this is also a priority for monitoring compliance with human rights. As a result of the progress made, it is hoped that a peaceful transfer of the territory to Croatia, but above all a peaceful co-existence between Serbia and Croatia, will be possible in the future. In this context, it should be stressed that the overall management of the UN Civil Police Operation is the responsibility of Austrian General Walter Fallmann. The Austrian executive was entrusted with the management of one of the most important UN missions. In the Western Sahara, the Austrian quota has been reduced from the last ten to now two executive officials due to the ceasefire process. The officials still in service will be able to obtain the necessary logistics in the event of a fresh start in the identification process, protect the parts of the electoral and registration commission still in place in the country and monitor the work of the local police. The Council of Ministers and the Main Committee have also decided that up to ten Austrian executives may participate in a humanitarian operation in Eastern Zaire. However, a start of this commitment is not yet foreseeable. Finally, I would like to say that all the previous operations have and always have a humanitarian component, whether through the support of aid organisations in their work or through their own, often privately motivated, actions of the officials themselves.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e6799	The Federal Minister has spoken. - Here you go.
ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e6820	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The consumer protection law in force for the adoption of the decision has committed itself to increasing the level of consumer protection in Austria. The draft is based on extremely lengthy and long-term preparatory work. One does not exaggerate if one of a ten-year-old soon This project is called 'genesis'. I am all the more pleased that the government template now, as I believe, has the best prospects of being adopted by all the political groups, and I am aware that it really needs some time. Today, we are in the middle of changing processes that need to be coordinated with economic conditions and thus consumer protection. In contrast to occasional concerns, new impulses come to a large extent also in the area of consumer protection from the legal development of the European Union, where, in particular since the Maastricht Treaty, the idea that the necessary liberalisation of economic life must be accompanied by an equally necessary consumer protection, because the condition of a functioning market is also an effective civil consumer protection law. It would be totally missing to understand consumer protection as a contrast to the free economy and to further liberalisation of business activities. On the contrary! Effective consumer protection is an essential complement, even a cornerstone of the free economic system. It is not a stimulant, but an essential condition for a fair balance of economic opposite interests. This includes, above all, the fact that the consumer is sufficiently informed of the product and its competition, i.e. transparency and freedom from misleading, and it is not pushed into transactions which he does not really seek and which are ultimately detrimental to him by over-reacting, psychological pressure or similar fairness. This objective, the achievement of a fair balance of interests, has been achieved by the Government template. It seeks, on the one hand, to limit various abuses in certain sectors of activity, for example by granting the right of withdrawal in the event of non-compliance with specific consumer ideas and by regulating housing rehabilitation contracts. On the other hand, the Government template also provides for new solutions for different areas of problem, for example for the common agreement of very high interest rates for default and for the well-known difficulties in the involvement of non-residents in the field of foreign credit liabilities. As already mentioned, we have decided against an all-or-no-non-solution, which generally explains the intercession of economically weak persons as inadmissible, as the German Judicatur makes it possible, rather in our project, to grant the judge the possibility, on the basis of differentiated criteria, to moderate or completely adopt the liabilities of the debtor or co-debt in a manner which is fair to the circumstances of the specific individual case. This represents both the legitimate interests of the credit economy and the protection of economically weak creditors. Finally, the Government template implements relevant developments in the European Union. In particular, reference should be made to the transparency offer for general terms and conditions and also to the limitation of the admissibility of so-called approval clauses for personal injury. However, the measures taken in the field of the General Civil Code, in particular to stop the unsolicited forwarding of goods already mentioned, could also be drawn up taking into account the very far-reaching draft of the EU Telecasting Directive. Overall, the Consumer Protection Act is a cornerstone in the efforts to raise the level of consumer protection in Austria. However, these efforts will not be completed, especially since it is very concrete to bring Austrian considerations to a further improvement of consumer protection into the European Union's activities in this respect and, above all, to continue the work carried out during the preparations of the Government's proposal on a guarantee reform, taking into account developments at European level in the national field. If we have made this suspension, this has not only been done with regard to the very contrary opinions in the assessment procedure, but also because our considerations are, on a number of essential points, contrary to the approaches of the European Union's already quite advanced consumer goods guarantee directive. In particular, there are some other approaches – apart from the guarantee period for movable contracts – which we would have planned primarily for the improvement of poor performance, while there is a reduction in prices. We therefore did not seem to be making any sense in making a national regulation which must be changed again in a year when it comes to a directive at European level, and I believe that this delay is therefore justified. Ladies and gentlemen! Builder contract law also serves to protect consumers, but its acquisition protection provisions are mandatory in favour of consumers. In view of the high amounts involved in buying an apartment or a rowhouse, there is also a particular need for protection provisions here. Even now, serious builders usually have legal assistance, which ensures as riskless a financial settlement as possible, but there are no legal provisions or minimum standards of protection in this area in Austria. In the case of the contractor contract, the risk of the acquirer providing intermediate services lies not only in the amount of these intermediate services, but also in the long period between the payment and the transfer of the completed contract item. The fact that security also requires effort for control and security measures and can therefore simply cost money is of course. However, it is always necessary to examine the relationship between the increased financial burden and the resulting increase in security. We have tried very hard not to cord the builders with a tight corset, but to make them choose as many safety models as possible. Each builder should be able to choose the most cost-effective variant for him, but at least one of these possibilities must be used in the interests of the acquirers. In doing so, we have taken advantage of the security and resolution models already introduced in practice, the costs of which are already being incurred today, so that the cost to be expected from a certain point of view, if such a one occurs at all, will be kept in the narrowest, quite reasonable limits and will only apply in addition to those where no safeguards have been provided to the purchasers – not at all rarely at all. For example, in the case of the basic book-keeping of the acquirers on the properties to be built, a legal assistance is currently usually provided as trustees, and the use of a civil engineer or expert to determine the progress of the construction is not new where the acquirer or the acquirer is financing by means of credit that are paid after construction progress. A number of visits by an expert on the construction site are sufficient for this progress control under the Council Plan, whose costs for the individual acquirer are really not serious. On the other hand, it ensures that the acquirer no longer makes advance payments to the builder than the actual increase in the value of the property or the share of the property to which he is entitled. If the builder wants to secure the acquirer through a bank guarantee, their costs depend very much on the credit quality of the builder. In the case of good credit quality, the cost is currently well below 1 percent per year of the insured sum. Finally, there is also the possibility of ensuring that the country's funding agencies ensure equal security for the acquirer and thus fulfil the construction's guarantee obligation. In any event, I am relieved that, in view of the unfortunately not too rosy economic expectations of the construction industry, the Baustander contract law is now being adopted and thus in future it is ensured that the often largest investment that a person makes in the course of his life, often with the benefit of all his savings, assistance from the family, taking up a loan, cannot be lost in any way in an existence-threatening way in the future. – Thank you for your attention.

In fact, Mr Schweitzer also knows that there are very positive aspects of genetic technology in medicine, for example, which should not be cut off. In our view, it is useful for individual companies to carry out research and take economic advantage of it, but also to be liable for those risks which are to be opened with it. We don't want any more, we don't want any less. I believe that this is a fair balance of interests. [[Congratulations at the Liberal Forum.]] [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: I can follow you in the field of medicine!]] Ladies and gentlemen! We have also suggested in our proposal – and this is what Mr Schweitzer knows, and he certainly agrees – that the environmental lawyers and similar bodies of the federal states, who are called for the protection of human health and the environment, should also have the opportunity to obtain a party position in the genetic engineering procedure, and should also receive a right of appeal to the courts of public law. We believe, by the way, that those preferences which are given, namely that the genetic engineering authorities have to decide within three months - at the earliest, but at least within three months - are actually not justified, because, in general, in the administrative procedure, the principle is that the decision can be made as quickly as possible, but for a long time within six months. We believe that this general regulation should also be sufficient in the field of genetic engineering. In addition, in this proposal, which is under discussion here, we have also made the proposal that the deadline for reporting accidents to the authorities should be shortened from two weeks to one week, and we have specified that we do not want any labelling of genetically modified products, but that it should also be clearly clear and understandable. This is a requirement which has already been decided unanimously here in the House in the report of the Committee of Inquiry, but then, as has been said, has not been implemented in the Genetics Act. The fact is: The whole House has become aware of such a clear and understandable labelling of genetically modified products, and we should finally implement them. Finally, it should be noted that we think it would be useful not only to have "God and the world within Austria" in the Committee on Genetics and Technology, but at least also to have representatives of the parliamentary clubs in this House to ensure that the information flow is maintained, in particular because it improves information opportunities – and no longer concerns the presence of a representative of the clubs in the genetic engineering committee. Information and control possibilities would also be improved by creating a genetic engineering register, as proposed in our proposal. All of this, ladies and gentlemen, in the end, in order to be able to enforce more easily those liability claims that will still be established. I don't want to hide anything. A crucial problem with the current liability schemes is that, as a single victim, one usually faces a very large company and has to bear costs that one cannot bear personally, especially if one has to prove everything itself until the end. We therefore think that, if there is a reasonable assumption that damage has been caused by genetically modified products or organisms, it would be useful to facilitate proof and to increase the obligation to participate by the company concerned in order not to impose too high a cost on the individual victim. That is why we call for proof to be easier for the injured and also for an association action. [[Congratulations at the Liberal Forum.]] It is against this solution that the argument has repeatedly been put forward that this is a reversing of the burden of proof and that there is no such thing in our liability law. I'm holding on, ladies and gentlemen: Our request does not deal with the reverse of the burden of proof, but is really just a relief, and we have not invented the model ourselves, but it is already enshrined in the Forest Act 1975. That is, where there are difficulties in proving, for example, where there are large areas of impaired forests, where one cannot decidicate, cannot say with scientific accuracy who is the real cause of these damage, these proofs apply. That is why this legal character has already been introduced in the legal system, and we think it would be time to apply it to the new technologies of genetic engineering. - Thank you.

This year alone, there have been five cases in which science has clearly erred: There is the Danish example of crossings, although it would never have been suspected that genetically modified hybrids would cross out. Another example is the incredible pollen flight in Scotland, where crossovers and the redevelopment of unplanned herbicides-resistant plants also occurred. In Texas the company Monsanto wanted to make wheat resistant to a certain worm, the bowlworm, but the entire first harvest was destroyed just by the pest against which plant should actually be made resistant. To this day, it is not clear what happened, except that the surrounding flora and fauna have changed considerably. A large research project – financed by tax funds – had to be launched in America to clarify this. Another example, probably the most prominent, is the soybean with the gene of the paranut of Ciba-Geigy, where it was only by chance that it could not be put on the market because the allergy would have caused significant problems. That is to say, this year alone, there were five examples where the entire science of the use of genetic engineering in this field has been mistaken. That field of science, which is extremely critical of this subject, is unfortunately being silenced by the government parties, even here in the House. ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e8903	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Today's law sets out for the first time a working time scheme for public health care institutions. So far, and even today, there has been a working time arrangement in these areas, which is not existing, i.e. without a limit, caregivers, nurses can be used there. And they will, unfortunately, too! Therefore, it was urgently necessary that we regulate, not because of the EU, but in the interests of the protection of workers and in the interests of patients, and make this regulation in such a way that it will apply to employees in private hospitals in the future as well as to employees in public hospitals and hospitals. The subject was certainly not easy to deal with. On the one hand, the discussion started because we had enormous congestion in the hospitals of public-law owners – that was rightly criticized – and on the other hand – you have already mentioned the issue of discussion in connection with Primarius Dr. Poigenfürst here – the desire and the need that the doctors have put forward that the working time regulations should become more flexible in private hospitals. This law takes both of us into account: For the first time, we have created workers' protection regulations and therefore patient protection regulations in both areas after very long negotiations. And I agree with all of you: There are also compromises in this law, as in almost every law that is being passed here, but I am in favour of this law, and I think it is right that it should be decided today. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] Even during the negotiations with the countries in recent weeks – it was only a part of the negotiations, with the countries there have been intensive negotiations for one and a half to two years, because they are part of the service providers – nothing has been changed by the law, and I will prove it to you afterwards. The basic situation is that we have regulations for working time, which provide that the maximum working day is 13 hours and that, on average, 17 weeks – this remains constant – weekly working hours must not exceed 48 hours. The draft already contained – including the request of the doctors and the co-workers in the hospitals – that one can make more flexible if there is no continuous use. Only then, if there is no continuous use, can these working hours be deviated from within the framework of the law. The legislative framework for business agreements is also laid down for this purpose. Negotiations on these operating agreements shall include, as it is new, the representatives of the group concerned. It's completely new. Only with their consent can there be deviations from working hours 13 hours per day or 48 hours per week at a 17-week average. Only with their agreement: I would like to hold this here again! For the first time, it has been established that the representatives of the group concerned should be involved in these negotiations, because there are, of course, other conditions in each hospital. Within hospitals, practically every department – therefore, this need is practical – must involve the speakers of the affected group. All of this was already in the draft, which you, Dr. Pumberger, would have voted in committee. Mr Oellinger has also confirmed this. At the time, there was still a very controversial point in the negotiations with the countries, namely whether gymnastics should also be included in this law. Many who did not wish to do so have referred to the EU Directive and explained that the EU Directive contains a provision which does not include doctors in education. I would like to stress here: Not all countries, but some countries have from the outset declared that they agree with the original version, for example Vienna, Burgenland and Carinthia; I only mention it as examples. And during these negotiations, it was disputed whether gymnastics should be involved, because it was argued that these were only doctors in education. We have been fully convinced, because the doctors are doing very important work in the hospitals, and it would have been incomprehensible if we had excluded them. I think that was one of the main issues in the discussions of the last few months on this law. The next issue in the negotiations with the countries which were not easy was that some countries wanted us to transfer this question to the countries as far as the public sector is concerned, and only the private hospitals continue to settle at the federal level. We have rejected this, too, because it would be impossible to give the responsibility to those who already declare in these negotiations that they do not want to comply with any provisions. That would therefore not have been responsible. That is why we have strictly explained that it remains federal. - That's done, too. On the question of willingness to call: The call was never approved by me with this law in any junk animation. Therefore, this law does not contain any provisions on the willingness to call, for the willingness to call is to be treated in such a way as it is to be treated in all other employment conditions. In this respect, there are obstetrical decisions that define what is actually working time, which is actually callability. This question is not addressed here, because if it is a quasi-responsibilities in which the doctors are actually not ready to call, but which is to be regarded as working time, then this would have to be dealt with after the above-mentioned decisions. I would like to point this out. As regards the derogations, as opposed to the first draft, You would have agreed to be inside. I would ask you to read these derogations carefully. If you wish to give your assent to the first draft in committee, you will be able to and will be able to agree to the second version. How are the derogations adopted? – The derogations are so restrictive that they can only be applied if not enough specialists are available in the near future. An example of an exception: It was established in the first draft - which is not possible in practice - that other regulations can be made by the end of 1997, now until the end of 1999, if, despite the invitation to tender, a specialist for the department in question cannot be found. This applies, of course, only to the field and to the extent that working time has to be regulated if a doctor can be found in spite of a call for tender. If a doctor can't be found for a hospital despite the invitation to tender – I think that will only be the case for a very short period of time – then it would have been impossible to ignore that in the law. Because then what should we have done? – We should have closed the hospital because we can't force anyone to commit. I am convinced that the wording: despite the invitation to tender no one has been found, and only to the extent that it is necessary, and that is limited in time, is a measure provided for and enforced by the countries and not by the practice. The provision that temporary derogations can be made is so restrictive that it cannot be used at all, as it stands in it: temporarily, if otherwise not feasible, precisely because of the lack of doctors. It is only temporary, if there is no continuous use, so the scheme is not affected by 13 hours of working time. It can only be temporarily derogated if there is an operating agreement on which the group concerned agrees and that of the individual. Someone must not have any disadvantages because he does not. A list of the workers concerned will be sent to the labour inspectorate, and the labour inspectorate – not the head of state or any other person – will then have to check, even if there is an agreement, whether the need is real and whether workers' protection is guaranteed in all cases. If this is not the case, the labour inspectorate will prevent such temporary arrangements. Therefore, there will be no over-examination under this law in the future, because these temporary transitional arrangements will also be subject to the control of the labour inspectorate. No country will be able to judge it, and any head of state who may at the moment claim to judge it with someone when he introduces these standards will be very wrong. For "temporary" means only if the need cannot be met otherwise, if the operating agreement allows, if the individual agrees and if the labour inspector acknowledges that this is still within the framework of worker protection. More restrictive derogations are certainly no longer possible. That is to say, after a very long time, the countries have actually accepted the need for protection of workers and patient protection in this area. We will ensure that the transitional arrangements are also dealt with very restrictively. This will also be done by the Labour Inspectorate – in the interests of the workers, in the interests of the doctors, in the interests of nurses, nurses and nurses and especially, of course, in the interests of the patients. I therefore hope that you will still be able to agree to this new law today. Thank you.

The Workers' Protection Act, which we are now dealing with, includes the prevention and prevention of risks to the health and safety of workers. Preventive workers' protection is aimed at avoiding accidents at work, occupational diseases and other occupational diseases and at enabling all workers to live and retire without occupational health disadvantages or disadvantages. There have probably always been conflicts and disputes at work. Recently, due to labour compression, but also by increasing unemployment, serious cases of psychoterrorism have become known at work. Mobbing refers to conflicts in which individuals are exposed to permanent pressure, isolation, slander, unjustified bad working conditions, and sexual harassment at work. Mostly, Mobbing is a valve to structural problems within a company. Professional stress through overload at the workplace and overload are often the feeding ground for disputes between irritated colleagues and colleagues – often from empty occasions. Professional stress is the best feeding ground for mobbing. Therefore, due to a permanent overload, there are also disputes within the group because of every small matter. The victims are now often systematically prepared in teamwork with the aforementioned strike lines and exploits. In most cases, the management will also experience these problems in the department and take measures that go from relocation to termination of the mobbing victim. Of course, people who are exposed to permanent mobbing have a very low self-esteem and are therefore mostly alone with their situation in operation. They no longer have sufficient personal and mental resources to survive a long period of conflict, and are mostly mentally and physically ill. The mobbers, on the other hand, are aware that in a conflict they always choose the more aggressive ones when weighing different behaviors. They actively ensure the escalation and continuation of conflicts, they usually show no guilt, but are, on the contrary, convinced of their indebtedness and are looking for the blame exclusively for the mobbing victim. – There are many examples for this; I cannot mention them for reasons of time. If mobbing is operated in a farm, this of course also has an economic impact. In particular, the service providers should start here, because a study in the United States found that on average, 40 percent of workers use their energy during the work process to defend themselves against hostile aggressions by others. If there is a mobbing situation in a farm – sometimes, however, it is also part of the strategy of corporate management to consciously use and boost the competition between employees, hoping to provoke an additional performance of the employees – an extremely unfavourable working environment, a hacking order that is modelled on the animal world, in which it is determined who has to bubble against you and who can be unpunished. There is already a book by Henry Walter entitled "Mobbing – Small War at Work" to resolve the conflict. In this respect, the trade union also provides support for the works councils in the form of initial advice and relevant information material. Finally, ladies and gentlemen: In order to avoid the phenomenon of Mobbing in principle or to create a new, viable operating environment after an overriding period of conflict, a new culture of dispute must be introduced in the company. A corporate culture that thinks and speaks out loud is the best early warning system against Mobbing. We Socialists are always particularly concerned about the protection of workers in areas which have not yet been regulated by law, because we are dealing with man first.

In a well-organised company, the next level will never be able to enjoy how the lower level terrorizes its own subordinate staff – I want to express it that way. This is neither for productivity nor for the operating climate nor for corporate culture, and therefore it is not good for business success. And here again there is a parallelity: Business management appreciates this neither for interpersonal reasons nor for reasons of efficiency. If you succeed in making living through regulations of this dual interest, then you will be successful. And that's why I'm going to tell you again: This law does not make this dual interest viable, but it makes this dual interest difficult, because it implies that one must protect the employee from an enemy boss – not from a perhaps negligent, but from an enemy. It is thus built up the fiction of opposition and a variety of interests. This law acts as if someone who would not have to comply with this law were a more successful entrepreneur and must therefore force him to do so through the law. And believe me: That's the real mistake of thinking! Therefore: If you use steering tools that accelerate this success, then you are successful with such a regulation; if you make taxative regulations, then you are not successful. A footnote to the Liberals' motion for a resolution: He's right and wrong at the same time. It is correct because, of course, it is unbearable that the federal officials are completely excluded from this law, so that a complete inequality is also given on cost issues and so on. It is wrong for him to do so, because a law that is still lacking in itself would be extended to the federal area. Therefore, it is a very classic case of weather yet: Neither do we want it to remain uneven as it is now, nor do we want this law to be extended to the federal area. We wish ourselves a real reform! - Thank you.

Haigermoser: That's why we give them up.]] This has brought clear and scientific economic benefits to our country, ranging from an approval of imports, a stabilisation of the expectation of exporters to, in the European comparison, extremely low interest rates. [[Abg. Haigermoser: Hallelujah!]] As you know, according to the provisions of the EU Treaty, 1. In January 1999 the third stage of economic and monetary union came into force. This was also confirmed by EU Heads of State or Government at the European Council meeting in Madrid in December 1995. The provisions of this Treaty are Austria due to the referendum of 12. 1 June 1994 and the accession to the European Union. ==References== On the part of the Austrian Federal Government, the provisions on monetary union have always been considered fully compatible with the Austrian stability objective, with additional economic benefits for Austria linked to the creation of a common currency area in the EU. In addition to the provisions of the EU Treaty itself, further discussions have taken place over the past year, the results of which are to be discussed or decided at today's meeting of the EU Council. This includes, in addition to the so-called Euro Regulation, the so-called EES II, which provides for stability by avoiding currency fluctuations between the euro and the non-participating currencies, and the Stability Pact, the objective of which is the concreteisation of the primary provisions already laid down in the EU Treaty. It ensures that Member States actually follow the requirement of budgetary stability as set out in the Treaty. This is also in line with the Austrian Federal Government's budgetary policy course, whose objective is not to pursue consolidation for the sake of fiscal consolidation, but to regain a future margin for an active economic policy through a sparing policy. These measures will also ensure that the euro will be as hard as schilling. This is not least ensured by the European Central Bank, whose mandate is aimed at maintaining price stability. Austria is already meeting essential conditions to meet and benefit from this stability-oriented course. This does not mean, of course, that economic policy will not have to be safeguarded and improved in the future by structural measures to ensure that the competitiveness of the Austrian economy is safeguarded and improved. The improvement of the budgetary framework will ensure that all the convergence criteria are met by the end of 1997, without, as with some of the proposals of the liberals, a collapse of the social security system. Monetary union only creates a positive framework on which to build nationally. In the field of monetary union, not only exchanges for private enterprises are missing, but it is ensured by the completion and completion of the internal market by a single currency of its consistency and consistency. This will ensure that the economies participating in it can operate under conditions similar to those within a State. This should have positive competitive effects for the European economy in the medium and long term, and therefore, of course, also for the Austrian economy. Especially important in the context of economic and monetary union decisions are the deliberations of the Heads of State or Government on measures of a pan-European employment policy. The Irish proposal on the outcome of the current Intergovernmental Conference largely incorporates the Austrian idea of an employment chapter, which is also widely accepted. A reasonable approach is chosen not to introduce additional formal criteria for economic and monetary union, but to establish a further framework for active employment policy. It is undisputed that there will be some areas of business in the banking sector, especially those which are currently geared towards the exchange of foreign exchange. In the EU as a whole and in non-EU countries, increased rationalisation efforts are currently taking place in the banking sector. However, as already stated by high-level bank representatives, our financial services companies are working to open up new business areas so that the transition to the euro and the restructuring of their business with the least negative impact on jobs in this sector remain. [[Abg. Haigermoser: No one believes that! Don't you read newspapers?]] This will be complemented by a positive labour market balance, which should be achieved through the increased competitiveness of the Austrian economy. However, the most important short-term effect will be that over a few years devalues of key trading partners can no longer endanger jobs of Austrian companies or Austrian workers. I am aware that this is not of interest to some, but I believe that most of the Austrian workers can achieve this objective of economic policy, namely job security. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! For the purposes of the Rules of Procedure of the National Council, the answers to each question shall be in writing. – I thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e13577	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This debate today is quite emotional, but we know that the move towards a single euro has already been completed, and it is on solid track. This has already been confirmed by the Heads of Government of the European Union. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Haigermoser.]] Through our budgetary policy, ladies and gentlemen, together with a consolidation programme, we are in this process and we are also meeting the conditions, so Austria is one of the core countries which from the beginning have joined the euro from 1st century onwards. It will be included in January 1999. What are these conditions? – There are five convergence criteria: first, the fiscal criteria, the budgetary discipline, the second, the monetary criteria. The fiscal criteria include the budget deficit of the public sector with a maximum of 3 per cent of GDP and, secondly, the debt of the public sector with a maximum of 60 per cent of GDP. We will meet these criteria, ladies and gentlemen, by the end of 1997. In 1998, it will be decided which countries will ultimately be involved in the process as a result of the results of 1997. I should now like to quote Mr Santer, President of the Commission: According to Santer, all speculations and hypotheses that are being established at the present time must not conceal the fact that the decision will not be taken until the spring of 1998. And on top of that: Until then, we can only have one goal in mind: to work towards compliance with the criteria so that as many countries as possible can participate. The President of the Commission also said: A shift in monetary union would have serious consequences, not only monetary but also political ones. It would take a whole generation to get this issue back up-to-date. - Quote. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Why is it so important that we are at the beginning? This is why it is so important to maintain the proven hard currency policy, the hard currency policy with which we have been very successful in recent years. Austria is thus one of the richest countries in Europe. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] But what are the other benefits? – Imagine that you order a goods whose delivery may take place four or five months later. It is therefore about the exchange rate risk, only at the exchange rate by the difference from purchase to selling price. Or another example: the investment in securities with foreign currency. – In concrete terms, an example: In 1985 the interest rate at the US dollar was about 12. 13 percent and the price was around 22 p. If you see where the dollar rate is due to the sharp decline today, then you need to notice: There are significant exchange rate differences. Another example: They take 100 000 S, travel from Austria through the 15 EU countries and lose only due to exchange rate losses of around 31,000 S. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Important is: With the euro, we also need a strong central currency for stabilising the world foreign exchange markets. We will not be able to afford to carry on exchange rate losses in the future. Therefore: It is about a currency exchange, but not about a currency reform.

ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e14803	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! After the somewhat exciting last minutes now on a subject that, as I think I can say, has become a kind of permanent burner, at least in the years since I was here in the House. As early as 1992, the Constitutional Court referred to the Austrian rules on the prohibition of night work for women. Nevertheless, unlike other European countries, this ban has not been repealed. In France and Germany, this happened in 1992. We have known for a long time that this form of the ban on night work, as it exists in Austria, is EU-absorbing and over the years, simply out of opportunities, has also made more and more exceptions to this ban for women. These exceptions are listed in Section 2 of this Federal Act, namely in 1969, 1972 and 1986. Finally, even this year, in 1996, there was a withdrawal in the area of bakers. In Austria, however, what still applies to bakery workers, fruit dealers, industrial workers and so on, so these women are not allowed to live, so Austrian doctors, pharmacists, nurses, cleaning women, telephone users, postmen and bar women have been living for a long time. For them this is the daily bread. I have the impression that Austrian women are only allowed to work at night, where they are urgently needed, and that the bans apply to those areas where women do not want them. In all these years, since I was aware of the problem, the ÖVP has always been verbally opposed to this night work ban for women, for example in 1992 – I quote from press releases –: "VP's outlawing women's night work." Or also 1992: "VP doesn't take ban on night work." But what has happened so far? It is quite clear that in September 1993 we made a petition for Tyrolean bakery workers, which was dealt with here in Parliament with number 81. On the basis of an opinion of the Austrian Trade Union Confederation, however, this petition was rejected in April 1994 by the ÖVP. As a result, we have then made motions for resolutions for resolutions in the XVIII, in the XIX. legislative period and now in the XX. Re-legislative period. But so far all our requests have been rejected – also with the votes of the ÖVP. The new motion for a resolution, which has been adopted since 14. It has not yet been dealt with in the Committee on Social Affairs. At the same time, however, in this year and also in 1995 there were press reports that this night work ban is being abolished. ==References====External links== March 1996 in the "Press" announced the abolition of the night work ban and a gender-neutral regime, which we also strive for. In June, the draft law should be in place. De facto, however, only had one draft. ==References====External links== March of this year. At the same time, there is also a draft for a so-called night work law for bakery workers – in turn an exception – for which the assessment period is on the 3rd. ==References== But it's done, it's decided nothing! Federal Minister! I believe that the indecisiveness and helplessness of this government also speak in these areas in which there are disadvantages for women. ParlaMint-AT_1996-12-13-020-XX-NRSITZ-00053_d7e14886	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This question will be discussed in the Committee on Social Affairs in the next few weeks, because today both government parties have submitted applications for it. Unfortunately, these requests by the government parties diverge in very important questions. Although there has already been an agreement at the presidential level between Hostash, Vergestnich, Maderthaner and Mitterbauer to save the social partners, the presidents of the Chamber of Commerce seem to have been re-buffed – perhaps by the party within the Chamber of Commerce, I do not know. We must live by the fact that the words or signatures of some of the social partners' associations are obviously nothing more valuable. That's very unfortunate. In any event, I think it is necessary that, if we generally release night work, we provide for protection measures for the women concerned. Unfortunately, it is in the real constitution that, as a general rule, I say this consciously as a representative of the male sex, in about 90, 95 percent of the families where children are there, the woman has to take care of, supervise and do more. It is therefore necessary either to give the woman the protection that she cannot be forced to work at night if family reasons are raised if children are present if she cannot accommodate the children at night, or if she is to be provided with appropriate facilities for the children. This should be regulated. It is also a difference whether a man or a woman is commuting between work and home at night, that is, it is also necessary to talk about the arrival and departure to the workplace. It is also necessary to provide for the possibility of the woman changing back to normal working time if, for family reasons, it is no longer possible for her to continue working at night without the threat of losing her job. Moreover, since almost all the physicians, especially those dealing with occupational medicine, say that night work is harmful to both men and women, we believe that time supplements should be introduced for every hour of work at night in order to enable better regeneration. As my previous speaker pointed out that night work for women had, for example, been introduced or released in the Federal Republic of Germany, I can remind you that this has been done very well with the corresponding time-limits for those affected. So we're not asking for anything that doesn't exist anywhere else. President Maderthaner did not realize his promise, which he had already given, in practice, in the matter of time supplements. However, I think it is urgently necessary that we put together, with regard to the question of the law on working time, what is more important about flexibilization and the like – I do not want to repeat my basic position on flexibilization here, which can be read in fifty protocols – and discuss this whole complex of issues if we want to come up with reasonable regulations together. But I am telling you with all the clarity: Just as there will be no flexibility at zero rates, there will be no night work for women at a zero rate, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen.

Mobbing in the establishments includes all levels in one holding, whether you are educated or not, whether it is the superior against the subordinate or only the subordinate, the employee against the other worker. This has nothing to do with education and criticism, but very probably with the conditions in this society. I recall that, obviously, only a few days ago, an investigation was presented that found what people are currently keeping from the business climate. They are less and less likely to see the pace in businesses becoming faster and faster, the burden on people is increasing. Working time also grows from its time dimension. It increases working time when you count overtime, it increases working time when you count the time off. It is not true that there is indeed a need for relaxation here. Accordingly, there are other causes that can lead to or prevent bad treatment in businesses with other people. And I think the causes are obvious: It is this ever-increasing stress, which the honourable Member says stupidly, that this stress, which takes place somewhere, certainly has nothing to do with the enterprises. The sufferings that people get – rheumatic illness, the diseases of the joint apparatus – they get themselves everywhere – at golf or elsewhere – but certainly not from the stress of work to which they are exposed in most of the time of their life when they do not sleep. Most of the time people spend in a fixed place is that at the workplace, and this is also the time when most of the loads are taking place, because people will certainly not be as sick as they are from work when they sleep. Ladies and gentlemen! I'm on an important problem with that. I believe that well-understood workers' protection, well-developed workers' protection should actually be a relief for businesses – not in the short term, perhaps not in the medium term, but in the long term. Let's look at the Austrian figures, Mr. Minister. They have been boldly presented by the honourable Member in a slightly distorted way today. He meant that the number of accidents at work in Austria was going back. Mr Minister, you will have to confirm to me that, unfortunately, they do not go back. In Austria, we have a relatively constant trend in the number of accidents at work over the decades, and this, ladies and gentlemen, is very different from other European countries. In Germany, they actually go back. For years, the number of accidents at work has not fallen by 1, by 2, by 3 per cent, but has fallen by 25 per cent to a longer period of 10 and 20 years. Not in Austria. Here we have a constant volume of work accidents. Who's paying for this? – If it does not pay the accident insurance, which is very often the case, it usually pays the local health phone, then this financial and this health burden falls into the general pots where it does not become visible. That's what I'm dealing with: It is necessary to develop a workers' protection which will become preventively effective, it is necessary to strengthen the equipment not only of the local health phone but also of the accident insurance institution with prevention. It is wrong what has happened in this direction over the last decades, that billions have repeatedly been expelled from the budget of accident insurance for budget financing and that no one has thought about what accident prevention, occupational disease prevention and prevention from occupational diseases would mean and whatever relief this would entail. That was not a topic in Austria. No one is interested in this, because this is already paid somewhere – through social security in general. Either through pension insurance or through health insurance – what do we care about? That is why it is important to strengthen preventive instruments and incentives in the farms as well. I therefore do not think that it is a mistake and that it is not a red tape that is being carried out in the operations evaluation - I know the Minister does not like the word - that the risk recognition and evaluation is carried out that the companies take this task seriously. I do not necessarily propose to the companies alone that they do not see this task. There is no one in Austria at the official level who would have had to deal with the subject in some way with serious and serious and would therefore really recognise the need to invest in this prevention, in this risk assessment also means that something should be put in here and that the insertion of resources would ultimately also be paid by a discharge in the health risks for the people in the farms. Germany spends 10 percent of its prevention budget in the area of professional cooperatives, i.e. the same breakdown as accident insurance. In Austria, we muffle in the accident insurance institution at a budget of 3 per cent for prevention in the businesses. Yes, that's not a comparison! As a social worker, I would like to point out another reason why I think it is worrying that the childcare check will be introduced. Whether parents house their children in high-quality childcare facilities or not would be completely left to the parents after the child care check was introduced. Especially for socially weaker families, the following problem arises because of their financial bottlenecks and their material need: They are faced with the question of whether to spend the money from the childcare check for the visit of their child in a kindergarten or to improve their overall financial situation. In other words: You will ask yourself the question: What's better for my child: To meet the basic existential needs of the whole family or to provide the child with help and support for his development through the kindergarten visit? I would also like to stress, in this context, the importance of the specialist staff in kindergartens in setting up preventive measures against the use of violence and the abuse of children. Please don't forget! I see this possibility at risk when parents no longer give their children in childcare facilities at all. In particular, we must help to improve their situation by improving and developing childcare facilities. Some comments on the mother-child passport. It is an instrument for identifying developmental defects and problems with young children in good time. In the framework of the mother-child passport screening, special attention is paid to children who, for example, face difficulties shortly after their birth and provide targeted medical and therapeutic measures as early as possible. For me as a child and youth spokesperson of SPÖ, the new regulation in connection with the mother-child-pass bonus is only a partial success. I consider it necessary that regular examinations be carried out with the children in the period after the second year of life, as far as possible until the start of school. Unfortunately, in this context, I see the danger that the motivation for parents to carry out these investigations will only be more limited in the future. I am already at the end of my remarks, but I still have the opportunity to wish you all in the House, despite these difficult three days and the somewhat frozen mood today, a happy Christmas and for the new year happiness, satisfaction and above all health! We can all need that! Thank you.

But the Minister did not say anything about it at all. We now want to clarify in a committee of inquiry what it has to do with the fact that this confession has come from the Ministry of the Interior and we call on you to accept this request. It can't be in your sense either that such a serious suspicion is simply not examined. You know very well that the Minister of the Interior does not actually answer specific questions. The Minister of the Interior is constantly speaking out when he is asked in concrete terms, even at other points, not only in terms of the letter bombs. We have only the only chance of clarifying the responsibilities of the Ministers of the Interior in a committee of inquiry. In any case, we are interested in the fact that the letter bomb terrorism is being resolved and that the Minister of the Interior does not give protection to terrorists.
